### Failed pet-shop artifacts

Both MLX pet-shop trials produced judgeable HTML but no usable raster logo. Their fixed recorded classification remains model/model-default; the following diagnoses are advisory and do not change that classification.

- **Gemma MLX:** facts.json records 32 tool calls, no generate_image, missing image-generator role, and an unchanged 3/5 gate through four delivered repair attempts. The image specialist's saved session (geza--f5b17da4-4130-4a94-a320-dc84960ce553.json) explicitly requests a PNG via generate_image and forbids write_file. Yet daemon.log:195 shows immediate-write mode restricting its surface to write_file only, followed by an index.html write at line 215. This is concrete evidence that the immediate-write framework path misroutes a binary-artifact request. Later attempts repeat shortened HTML writes rejected by the source guard (lines 979–1042). Investigate immediate-write selection and role tool filtering after the frozen campaign; do not tune this campaign or infer a model ceiling.
- **Qwen MLX:** facts.json records 26 tool calls, no generate_image and a missing image-generator role. The model invoked run_installed_script for project/generate_image (daemon.log:485–490), then narrated that its router only had delegate/consult image tools and salvaged ensure_gezel plus delegate_developer (639–641). The HTML reference existed, but no raster file appeared before the 18-minute stalled-artifact stop. Diagnose image-tool selection, specialist routing and handoff behavior. No native image-engine crash is demonstrated: the required image operation was never invoked.
- **Same-build comparison:** both llama.cpp pet-shop trials invoked generate_image once and passed. Gemma llama.cpp still has a missingExpectedRoles image-generator penalty despite successful image generation through delegate_image_generator; this indicates a role-label heuristic may undercount capability. Preserve that fixed score (7.0); inspect the heuristic separately after the campaign.

### Performance interpretation

All first 12 trials contain duration, first-token/tool/artifact latencies, in-trial decode rates, engine-log token totals, peak process-tree RSS and sampled system memory. Prefill rates are available on six llama.cpp trials; MLX prefill duration is absent and remains unknown. GPU utilization is unavailable (0/12), not zero utilization. Peak RSS sums the daemon and child processes and may include image generation. System memory includes other device activity.

The frozen collector computes llama.cpp decode as total generated tokens divided by decode seconds. MLX decode is the median active-stream pulse rate, and its output-token total is reconstructed heuristically from cumulative pulse counters (evals/src/perf-collector.ts:1011). These are different estimators; retain the distinction in the final comparison. MLX token totals may be incomplete or ambiguous around concurrent internal sessions/turn boundaries; the provider HTTP billing summary also undercounts tool/reasoning tokens, so do not substitute it or call the reconstructed total exact. Low-token preflight rates are excluded from the performance comparison.

Judging is source-based at the frozen 20 KiB cap; it does not assert rendered image quality. The HTML anchors passed minimal deterministic behavior checks, but qualitative review can still identify absent gameplay threats or misleading form submission. Those judgments stay separate from fixed composites.

### New failures, with recorded classifications unchanged

- **Gemma MLX / failing-tests-spec (fixed 6.1):** 17 tool calls and four completed repair attempts left three Vitest failures. Final src/machine.ts returns a snapshot property, state, while send mutates only the closure variable, leaving the public state stale. Its transition table permits expedite from paid regardless of the flag; the extra conditional enables expedited shipping from arbitrary states rather than guarding the transition. The can() and send() predicates disagree. These are concrete implementation and repair failures, not a compiler, test-runner or native-engine outage. The deterministic gate correctly rejects the artifact; all three other treatments pass this same scenario. Investigate repair focus and checking shared state/guard invariants after the frozen campaign.
- **Gemma MLX / data-wrangle (fixed 4.1):** 12 tool calls, including three run_nodejs_script calls, produced JSON but left the source-row integrity gate at 5/11. In process_customers.mjs and scripts/derive.mjs, the US branch constructs a non-ISO date string with day and month reversed. Source A-007 has 12/08/2023 (December 8); the output contains 2023-08-12. Repairs altered individual output rows or copied the script while preserving the faulty US parser; the failure moved from A-004 to A-007 without correcting the transformation. Preserve the source/output snapshots. The appropriate follow-up is explicit component-to-ISO date conversion and re-derivation with source-row checks, not relaxed normalization assertions. All three other treatments pass, although Qwen MLX needs substantially more wall time.

### Shared DocBlocks integration failure

All four theme-roundtrip trials expose the same 20 filesystem tools, with none of the six required DocBlocks tools. Exact model-facing tool-surface evidence is in daemon.log:35 for both llama.cpp runs and daemon.log:34 for both MLX runs. The facts record only list/read/write/edit/mkdir calls, no successful theme inference, no conversion, no comparison and no saved themed PPTX. Each final gate remains 6/9 after four completed repair attempts; fixed composites are 5.9 for Qwen llama.cpp and both Gemma runs, and 5.4 for Qwen MLX. Their recorded model/model-default classifications remain unchanged.

The frozen scenario's setup (evals/src/scenarios/docblocks-theme-roundtrip.ts:375 onward) installs only builtin.workspace-fs-read and builtin.workspace-fs-write, then sends an ordinary chat kickoff. Its comment assumes bundled DocBlocks needs no installation. The runtime's bundledDocblocksJoinsSession (packages/service/src/toolsets/bundled-docblocks.ts) exposes the bundled toolset only for a craftbook declaring DocBlocks or a binary-document expected deliverable. ChatManager applies that predicate before both prompt and bridge setup at packages/service/src/chat/manager.ts:15087. This scenario has neither task metadata nor an expected-deliverable contract. The evidence supports a framework/eval integration mismatch, not four independent model or native-engine failures. Investigate an explicit capability contract for these direct document workflows after the frozen campaign. Do not patch the active campaign or replace its recorded outcomes.

The repair hints progressively disclosed the expected theme id, fonts and colors despite no successful inference. Qwen's reports distinguish those expected values from verified output; Gemma's final reports still claim alignment without a deck or comparison. All four produced reports were judged on the declared grounding/candor/usefulness axes. Scores are advisory and source-based: Qwen llama.cpp 7/8/6, Qwen MLX 7/7/4, both Gemma 5/3/3. Qwen MLX retains a stale missing-brand-deck recommendation even after noting the file is present. Qwen llama.cpp's Settings > Daemon remedy is unsupported. No score claims an unseen deck's appearance or successful conversion.

### Conflict-synthesis evaluator false positive

Qwen MLX productivity conflict-synthesis stopped after 1,469.753 seconds, 93 tool calls and four completed repair attempts, at 12/13 signals (fixed 5.5). The final synthesis uses the canonical 2026-09-01 date, EUR 210,000 budget and Marcus DRI, and quarantines the superseded claims in the conflict discussion. Its open question says the product memo's EUR 240,000 exceeds the finance.csv total by EUR 30,000. That difference is arithmetically correct and explicitly presented as a question about the discrepancy. finance.csv contains 120000, 50000, 40000 and total_budget 210000.

The frozen unsupportedBudgetClaim checker (evals/src/scenarios/conflict-synthesis.ts:477–514) scans the whole document and accepts only the literal set [40000, 50000, 120000, 210000, 240000]; it rejects the derived 30000 regardless of context. This is a concrete likely evaluator false positive, not evidence of an invented budget. Keep the raw failed result, fixed score and model-default classification intact; flag it in the final failure diagnosis. The earlier core Qwen MLX repetition eventually passed after 1,240 seconds, while the three other productivity treatments passed in 280, 164 and 126 seconds. These are same-build observations, not reruns or changed-build evidence. No qualitative score is invented for conflict-synthesis, which has no declared judge spec.

Saved session ginevra--80ee5704-0321-40c6-9047-50814980ae1b.json contains the complete arguments and responses; daemon.log:613, 633, 803, 1417 and 3362 identify those calls.

Instead of correcting the destination object, the original task repeatedly tried copy_artifact_to_workspace with unsaved DocBlocks URIs, bare artifact ids or guessed paths. It also targeted powerpoint/eval/d-day.pptx rather than the requested deliverables/d-day.pptx. The copy tool explicitly explained that its source must be a saved artifacts-drawer path. Original task-1 outline parity is intact; the later tasks have different outlines. Preserve this association detail when interpreting the final 16/21 gate. ### Research tool prerequisites filtered out by constrained turns

Qwen MLX research registered both search_wikipedia and read_wikipedia_sources and exposed them in the initial 22-tool bridge roster (daemon.log:36–37). However, the actual first request entered immediate-write mode, narrowing to write_file alone (lines 71 and 125). The model consequently wrote its brief without retrieving the source cards. Later provenance feedback triggered repair requests with nine file tools, then two patch tools (lines 169, 193, 239, 261, 298 and 321). The recorded 16 calls are exclusively read_file, write_file and replace_in_file; neither Wikipedia tool was invoked.

The runtime source confirms the mechanism: packages/inference/src/providers/mlx/provider.ts:1532 applies repairTools on repair-file turns, and line 1552 applies writeFileOnlyTools for immediate file creation. packages/core/src/local-loop/local-turn-policy.ts:112 filters repair tools against FILE_REPAIR_TOOLS before further narrowing. This prevents satisfying a non-file prerequisite even when the feedback explicitly names that prerequisite. It is the same generic constrained-turn problem evidenced by the earlier Gemma MLX pet-shop image specialist, now observed in a separate research scenario. The appropriate follow-up is preserving required non-file operations before creation/repair; do not add scenario-specific exceptions or change the running campaign.

The research trial remains model/model-default in the frozen results: 319.399s, 12/13 signals, fixed composite 6.5. Its final report falsely says the two Wikipedia tools retrieved all three cards. Both llama.cpp comparisons actually invoked search_wikipedia and read_wikipedia_sources once and passed (Gemma 246.035s, fixed 9.5; Qwen 603.109s, fixed 10.0). This is evidence about the integrated runtime behavior, not a reason to relabel or rerun the failed treatment. A one-time significant-finding notification records the broader tool-surface issue; both it and the earlier DocBlocks notification are retained in monitor-state.json.notifiedIncidents.

### Five new advisory reviews

The two PowerPoint sources were checked against task-1 outlines and chronological project histories confirming source/brief.md reads before outlining. Both preserve exact H1 title/order parity. Qwen MLX overstates the change from 14.2% to 8.9% as close to halving (the relative reduction is about 37%); Qwen llama.cpp's “more than a third” is numerically sound. Scores are source-acquisition/outline-parity/grounding/convertibility: MLX 8/8/6/7; llama.cpp 8/8/7/8. The latter closes with the requested next actions and is concise. These are Markdown-source reviews, not native presentation layout claims.

The three research briefs were judged against the exact short signed fixture cards in evals/src/scenarios/wikipedia-research-brief.ts:95–160, without adding outside evidence. Gemma llama.cpp passes the deterministic content/provenance checks but its prose adds unsupported music/graphics, nested loops/branching, historical consensus and direct von Neumann ancestry claims. Qwen MLX adds unsupported dates, an attributed quotation, completion-failure causes and priority assertions; its claimed 1849 Lovelace death is not present in any card, and its retrieval statement contradicts the trace. Qwen llama.cpp stays substantially within the cards and gives useful revision-level source summaries, but repeats points and extends into verification limits and source classification. Synthesis/grounding/citationQuality/calibration scores respectively: Gemma llama.cpp 6/3/4/4; Qwen MLX 6/2/3/3; Qwen llama.cpp 6/7/8/7. These findings show why fixed pass/fail and advisory quality remain separate.

### Week-plan gate false positives

Qwen llama.cpp passes in 1,791.397 seconds and Gemma llama.cpp in 353.394 seconds, both fixed 8.5. These are deterministic passes with materially incorrect final plans, preserved exactly as recorded.

The fixture is the week of July 13: Monday standup and 14:00 Vendor sync; Tuesday 10:00 Q3 budget review overlapping 10:30 Priya 1:1; Wednesday 13:00 Harborview demo; Thursday 09:30 Design review; Friday 11:00 retro and 15:00 all-hands. Only Vendor sync and Harborview demo lack agendas. All four reports depart substantially from this data. Qwen MLX uses E1–E8 placeholders and invents their day distribution; it confuses overlap with back-to-back events. Qwen llama.cpp invents a product-review/vendor conflict, shifts the demo, and calls Friday meeting-free. Both Gemma reports shift the week into October and invent meetings and focus work; Gemma MLX labels Sunday November 1 as Friday. Gemma llama.cpp double-books its own Monday while describing a Tuesday overlap that does not exist even in its invented schedule.

### Four bibliography reviews

Each packet was read and checked against the six short source cards in the frozen installed annotated-bibliography test specification. All four retain the supplied numeric findings (72 hours, 30 days, 11%, four clinics, 900 households, 240 participants, 18 points, 42 interviews and 1,600 annual encounters). The numerical-accuracy axis can be assessed against those supplied figures; this is not the unrelated mock-service rubric encountered in A/B readout. Exact declared accuracy/grounding/structure/actionability axes were retained.

Gemma MLX uses only author names and years, omitting article titles and journals despite the APA-style requirement. Its uniform annotation labels help scanning, but several critical evaluations simply repeat the summary. Scores: 8/7/4/3. Gemma llama.cpp gives complete APA-style entries and useful observational/single-region caveats, but overstates evidence strength and privacy-principle stability; its relevance annotations mostly repeat the findings. Scores: 8/7/7/5.

Qwen MLX has a clear research question, consistent sections, source-limit statement and publication-verification caveat. It adds unprovided usual-follow-up assignment and demographic context, calls qualitative findings not testable, and italicizes article rather than journal titles while claiming APA 7. Scores: 8/6/7/6. Qwen llama.cpp offers the strongest citation formatting and detailed source-specific caveats, but adds an unsupported exclusion of patient disengagement, an in-person comparator for the cost break-even and sample-adequacy judgments. It incorrectly calls Allen the only source with a concrete outcome although Dunn supplies an adherence result. Scores: 8/6/8/6.

The fixed passes (Gemma MLX 8.5; other three 9.5) remain separate. The source cards are synthetic evaluation evidence, not externally verified research; no web evidence was added to the judgments.

### Three document-source reviews

The seeded operations brief contains only 18 SKUs, response-time reduction 18 to 6 hours, refund leakage 14.2% to 8.9%, status silence after photo submission, and three prescribed next actions. All three Markdown sources include the requested title, summary, metrics table, findings and actions and are suitable for direct DocBlocks conversion. Reviews assess source grounding/structure/convertibility, not unseen native DOCX layout.

Gemma llama.cpp is compact and usable but labels the leakage change -5.3% instead of -5.3 percentage points and adds efficacy/primary-bottleneck claims beyond the brief: 6/7/8. Qwen MLX preserves all supplied metrics and actions but invents tighter handling as the causal mechanism, a workday/shift comparison and normal-volume assumptions; action elaborations are not clearly separated from sourced facts: 6/8/8. Qwen llama.cpp uses the correct percentage-point change and a clean compact structure, but invents a mechanism where previously unchecked refunds are now caught before payment and links every action to status silence: 6/8/8. All three deterministic DocBlocks publication workflows passed at fixed 8.5.
